# T0-39: Visa and Work-Permit Dossier: UK, Ireland, Germany, Netherlands, France and Switzerland

This dossier maps the legal routes for a GenAI professional to work in the United Kingdom, Ireland, Germany, the Netherlands, France and Switzerland, with rules as in force on 2026-11-13 and the research date of 2026-11-14. Source references appear as [S#], the global numbers in the Sources & Evidence Ledger (S1 to S155); local notes identifiers are not used. Grades: **A** an official page or text was read; **B** a secondary summary, law-firm or vendor page, or an extract or snippet of an official page; **C** commentary, title or snippet only; **D** unverified, including the coordinator's priors and memory-based figures; **E** absence (searched, not found); **M** computed by the researcher from cited inputs; **Gap** not verified. Round 1 sources were retrieved on 2026-11-13 and round 2 sources on 2026-11-14; the brief's temporal anchor was 2026-11-11. Many official domains refused automated fetching (among them the GOV.UK visa processing-times page, gesetze-im-internet.de, Legifrance, fedlex.admin.ch, swissvotes.ch and the IND tariffs page), so some official pages were read only as search snippets or through a summarising fetch tool. Those figures are graded B or lower unless the notes show that the primary page was read. Where a later round in the notes overrides an earlier reading, the later reading is used and the change is stated.

## Prior

| # | Proposition | Confidence | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | UK Skilled Worker: since 22 July 2025 the general salary threshold for new applications is £41,700 a year, the minimum skill level is RQF 6, and the Immigration Salary List has been replaced by a Temporary Shortage List. | 0.75 | D |
| P2 | Germany: the 2026 EU Blue Card salary floors are €50,700 (standard) and €45,934.20 (shortage, ICT and new-graduate cases), set at 50% and 45.3% of the pension-insurance contribution ceiling (€101,400 a year for 2026), and the 2027 floors are published in December. | 0.80 | D |
| P3 | Netherlands: the 30% ruling is scheduled to change from 1 January 2027 to a flat 27% for up to five years, and the 2026 gross monthly Highly Skilled Migrant threshold for applicants aged 30 or over is about €5,900 (exact figure not recalled). | 0.55 | D |
| P4 | Ireland: Critical Skills Employment Permit holders can apply for Stamp 4 after 2 years; the 2025 salary floors were €38,000 (Critical Skills Occupations List) and €64,000 (other occupations); the 2026 figures are not recalled. | 0.65 | D |
| P5 | France: the EU Blue Card salary floor is about 1.0 times the average gross annual salary (roughly €55,000–€60,000), separate from the "salarié qualifié" Talent Passport, and French-language requirements for multi-year and resident cards rose from 1 January 2026 (A2 for the multi-year card, B1 for the resident card). | 0.50 | D |
| P6 | Switzerland: the 2026 federal quotas for third-country nationals were 4,000 B permits and 4,500 L permits (8,500 in all, plus UK-specific quotas); there is no EU Blue Card; the "No to 10 million Switzerland" initiative was due to a popular vote in 2026. | 0.55 | D |

## Research Question

**Decision question.** For a GenAI professional who wants to work in the United Kingdom, Ireland, Germany, the Netherlands, France and Switzerland, which legal routes to work exist at the research date, what does each require, cost and take, how likely is an employer to use it for an AI role, and what leads to permanent residence? The answer informs decision D-007 and the knowledge-base output `career/11-tracks/visa-and-relocation.md`. Because a reader acts on it, accuracy and dates matter more than breadth.

**Sub-questions.**

1. Which routes exist in each country for a skilled tech worker: employer-sponsored, talent or self-sponsored, points-based, job-seeker, graduate, intra-company, founder, remote-work and India-specific schemes?
2. For each route: eligibility, salary threshold, processing time, fees, dependants, path to permanent residence, with source and grade.
3. Practical likelihood: how often sponsorship is used for AI and software roles, and where the official data stop.
4. What is pending, contested or changed since 2025-01-01, with dates and status.
5. Indian nationals: caps, bilateral schemes, consular waits, document attestation, police clearance, degree recognition and TB or medical rules, with gaps stated plainly.

**Reading of the brief applied.** The brief's KNOWN claims K1 to K7 were treated as hypotheses to test, not as facts to repeat; each has a verdict in the Repository Deltas section. References to the knowledge base's own sessions were not treated as evidence. The question type is lookup (official rules) followed by evaluative (practical likelihood): official rules are given first and practical likelihood second. No approval odds are estimated for any individual. The brief's anchor date (2026-11-11) was read as the temporal anchor; the research date is 2026-11-14.

**Scope.** The brief's scope list was applied in full: employer-sponsored, talent, points, job-seeker, graduate, intra-company, founder (briefly), remote-work (briefly, including foreign employers), India-specific schemes, dependants, settlement, language, degree recognition, employer obligations, processing and fees, quotas and lotteries, and changes since 2025-01-01. Out of scope: tax advice beyond one-line pointers, asylum and family-only routes, and routes closed to skilled workers. Coverage is uneven. The UK and Germany are the most complete. The Netherlands, Switzerland and France have the largest gaps, and Ireland's permit-statistics files could not be read (see the Absences section).

**Method.**

- **Two rounds.** Round 1 (retrieved 2026-11-13) produced seven country notes files, each with a source ledger, route census, status table, K-verdicts and a test of the priors. Round 2 (retrieved 2026-11-14) re-verified the load-bearing figures and attacked the priority gaps: Indian nationals, document rules, permit statistics and degree recognition. In this dossier "the notes" means these working files. Round 2 overrides round 1 where they conflict.
- **Languages and queries.** Searches were run in English and, for national official pages, in German, Dutch and French (for example "Blaue Karte EU 2026 Gehaltsgrenze", "highly skilled migrant Netherlands" with Dutch terms, and "décret 2026-308 carte bleue européenne"). Each notes file lists its queries.
- **Falsification searches.** Each K-claim was tested with at least one search aimed at a disconfirming result. Examples: UK K2 searches for published endorsement pass rates; German K5 searches for the IT-without-degree route and fast-track timing; French K7 searches for a one-year minimum contract; Dutch searches for evidence that the highly-skilled route is fast; Irish searches for backlog reports; Swiss searches for criticism of the route.
- **Sources.** The ledger holds 155 source IDs (S1 to S155; S155 is an unused placeholder). Access grades follow the definitions above; blocked pages are recorded as Gap, not as evidence of absence.
- **Who read what.** Smaller-model sub-agents did the page reading and first-round extraction. Round 2 independently re-checked the load-bearing figures (UK thresholds and fees, German floors, Irish and French thresholds, the Swiss quota) against the source text where a source was available.

**Limits.**

- Access: several official pages were refused (listed under Absences). Statute text was often not read: the German §18g was read only through the BMI notice; the French CESEDA text was not opened; the Swiss AIG and VZAE text was not reached.
- No nationality-level permit counts for India were extracted. The Irish permit-statistics files and the UK Home Office tables could not be read by the fetch tool.
- No employer-level data on sponsorship for AI roles exist in any source read.
- Changes after 2026-11-13 are not captured. Several items are pending, including the UK settlement Statement of Changes, the German 2027 floors and the Dutch 2027 norms.
- The dossier is a set of pointers to official sources. It is not legal advice.

## Recommendation

These are decision rules and pointers to official sources, not legal advice. Check the official text on the filing date: German and Dutch salary norms change on 1 January; Irish thresholds changed on 2026-03-01; UK rules change by Statement of Changes; Swiss quotas change by federal decision. No approval odds are estimated for any individual.

### Decision rules by reader type

| Reader | Route to test first, and the rule | Failure mode |
|---|---|---|
| **(a) Mid-level engineer with an offer** | **UK:** Skilled Worker, if the job is RQF 6 or above and coded to an ICT SOC code (for example 2134 or 2139), and pay is at least the greater of £41,700 and the going rate (2134: £54,700; 2139: £52,300, as shown on the page updated 2025-07-22) [S1], [S3]. **Germany:** EU Blue Card at €50,700 gross, or €45,934.20 where the bottleneck or IT conditions are met, with a recognised degree or the IT-experience test [S94], [S85]. **Netherlands:** highly-skilled migrant route if the employer is a recognised sponsor and pay meets €5,942 (age 30 or over); otherwise EU Blue Card at the same standard amount [S113], [S106]. **Ireland:** CSEP if the role maps to a CSOL entry (most likely SOC 2136) and pay meets €40,904 with a relevant degree, or €68,911 without [S61], [S64]. **France:** carte bleue européenne at €59,373 gross a year, with a diploma or 5 years' experience [S127], [S134]. **Switzerland:** L or B permit, subject to the federal quota and the priority test [S136], [S137]. | **UK:** offer below the going rate for the code, or the role coded to a Table 1a occupation (for example 3544, RQF 3 to 5), which fails the RQF 6 test [S2]. **Germany:** salary below the floor on the filing date, or degree recognition not complete. **Netherlands:** pay below the monthly norm (holiday allowance excluded), or the employer is not recognised. **Ireland:** the title (for example "data scientist", "AI engineer" or "ML engineer") does not map to a CSOL entry, so the employer falls back to a GEP with the labour-market needs test, the 50:50 rule and quota-closure risk [S64], [S69]. **France:** salary below the floor on the filing date; the reference changes each year. **Switzerland:** quota exhausted, or refusal on the priority or salary test. |
| **(b) Senior engineer or researcher without an offer** | **UK:** Global Talent (digital technology), where endorsement comes first and no job is needed [S33], [S42]; Global Talent (researcher or academic leader) if the work is research [S36]. **Germany:** Opportunity Card for a job search of up to 1 year, with at least 6 points and blocked funds of €1,091 net a month [S87]. **Netherlands, Ireland, France (beyond the researcher category, not assessed), Switzerland:** no job-seeker route was verified in scope. | **UK:** endorsement refused on the evidence (three letters from authors who have known the applicant's work for at least 12 months; up to 10 documents of up to 3 A4 sides, with CV and letters outside the cap) [S43]; the £561 endorsement fee is not refunded (refund policy not checked, Gap). **Germany:** fewer than 6 points, or funds not blocked. **Netherlands, Ireland, Switzerland:** no route exists in the opened sources until an offer is in hand; do not plan a move on an unverified route. **France:** a host agreement is not available for the researcher category. |
| **(c) Recent graduate** | **UK:** Graduate route, if the applicant holds or held a UK Student visa and completed a UK course. Apply on or before 2026-12-31 for 2 years, or from 2027-01-01 for 18 months [S5], [S17]. **Germany:** EU Blue Card at the reduced floor for graduates within 3 years of graduation; the figure is contested (S86 reads 45.3% of the ceiling, EUR 45,934.20; S84 gives no figure) [S84], [S86]. **Netherlands:** zoekjaar hoogopgeleiden if the institution meets the top-200 test, then highly-skilled migrant at €3,122 (reduced) [S104], [S102]. **Ireland:** CSEP at the €36,848 recent-graduate threshold if the qualification is within 12 months and the role is on CSOL [S70]; or GEP at €34,009 for Irish Level 8+ graduates [S69]. **France and Switzerland:** no graduate route verified in opened sources. | **UK:** the window lapses before the 2026-12-31 date; the Graduate route cannot be extended and does not count towards settlement [S5], [S7]. **Germany:** the reduced figure is not confirmed in the official text read; verify before relying on either figure. **Netherlands:** the institution is not in the top 200 in at least 2 of the 3 rankings, or the Nuffic valuation is not complete [S104]. **Ireland:** the 12-month window lapses before an offer, or the role is not on CSOL; the Stamp 1G graduate programme is for Stamp 2 holders and is not available to someone without prior Irish study (2017 revision, possibly superseded) [S77]. **France:** salarié qualifié needs a master-level diploma and €39,582 a year, which a new graduate may not meet [S134]. **Switzerland:** the quota-exempt Master's and PhD route exists only at consultation stage; its in-force date is unverified (Gap) [S142], [S153]. |

### Failure modes for recommended routes

The failure mode for each recommended route is given in the reader table above. Across all routes, the most frequent failure is a salary or title that fails a threshold on the filing date (the UK going rate, the German floors, the Irish CSOL title match, the French reference salary) and a route depending on an employer offer that the employer cannot or does not sponsor.

| Option | Weighed for | Outcome | Reason (with source) |
|---|---|---|---|
| UK High Potential Individual (HPI) | Graduates with an overseas degree, no sponsor | Rejected for most readers | Degree must be from an institution on the Global Universities List on the award date; the 2026 list (first published 2026-11-01, 82 institutions, awards to 2027-10-31) has no Indian institution [S38] (A); HPI is not a settlement route [S39] (A); fee £880 as shown [S52] (A, effective date unverified) |
| UK India Young Professionals Scheme | Indian citizens aged 18 to 30 | Kept as an option for eligible applicants only | Ballot; 3,000 places in 2026; no further ballots in 2026; no 2027 ballot announced; visa fee £340 (updated 2026-04-08) [S44] (A) |
| UK Innovator Founder | Founders with an endorsed business idea | Not recommended for this reader | Requires endorsement by an approved body; visa £1,357 outside the UK; endorsement £1,000 plus £500 per meeting [S40] (A, extraction; fee page blocked on re-read); settlement after 3 years [S40] |
| UK Global Talent (researcher or academic leader) | Senior researchers without an offer | Recommended for research work only | Fast track "usually within 2 weeks" with an eligible appointment; otherwise "usually within 5 weeks" (page dated 2024-10-21) [S36] (A); the UKRI Endorsed Funder pathway, announced 2026-08-06, has no stated effective date [S48] (A) |

| Germany Opportunity Card (points route) | Job search without an offer | Recommended for reader (b) | The only job-search route found in Germany; points and funds rules at [S87] (A); issue numbers unverified (D) |
| Germany IT route without a degree | Experienced IT workers without a degree | Included as an alternative | Two routes with BA approval at €45,934.20 (3 years in 7) and €45,630 (2 years in 5), both 2026 [S85] (A, graphic); settlement path not verified |
| Germany Blue Card (standard versus reduced floor) | Employed engineers with an offer | Recommended for reader (a) | Floors and settlement timing confirmed on official or municipal pages [S94], [S89]; graduate reduced rule contested (S84 versus S86) |

| Netherlands single permit with UWV work permit (TWV) | Employer without recognised-sponsor status | Not verified; not recommended | Searched only; UWV and GVVA pages were seen in search and not opened (E) |
| Netherlands zoekjaar versus direct highly-skilled migrant route | Graduates | Zoekjaar for qualifying graduates only | Zoekjaar is 1 year, cannot be extended, and needs a qualifying institution [S104] (B) |
| Netherlands job-seeker route for experienced workers | Senior engineers without an offer | Not found | No route found in scope; zoekjaar applies only to graduates or to research in the Netherlands within 3 years [S104] (B) |
| Ireland General Employment Permit (GEP) | Roles not on CSOL | Fallback only | Needs a labour-market needs test, the 50:50 rule and a quota where one applies; quotas closed or nearly full for several roles in 2026 [S63] (A) |
| Ireland Stamp 1G graduate programme | Graduates | Not recommended for most readers | Applies only to Stamp 2 holders; 2017 revision possibly superseded [S77] (B) |
| France salarié qualifié (Talent Passport) | Master-level holders with a salary offer | Alternative to the EU Blue Card | Gross annual salary at least €39,582 [S134] (B, summary); the talent categories beyond the EU Blue Card were not verified in round 2 |
| France talent categories (researcher, company creator, cultural professions, others) | Varied | Listed, not recommended | Thresholds appear on the Justice.fr summary [S134] (B, truncated read); processing as prefecture deadlines, not published service standards |
| France French Tech Visa | Start-up founders | Not assessed | No official page opened; only commercial pages seen (E) |
| Switzerland L permit versus B permit | Employers with an offer | L or B, subject to quota; B for multi-year stays | Quotas 2026: 4,000 L and 4,500 B [S136] (A); multi-year B permit subject to integration criteria [S145] (A) |
| Switzerland EFTA–India agreement | Natural persons from India | Not recommended until the annex is read | In force from 2025-10-01 [S144] (A); the Swiss commitments for professionals are in an annex not read in treaty text [S149] (B) |
| Switzerland graduate exemption (Swiss-university Master's and PhD) | Recent graduates | Not recommended as a current route | Consultation opened 2021-10-27 [S142] (A); in-force date unverified; the earlier "from 2025" reading is not supported [S153] (C) |

## Detailed Findings

### United Kingdom

This part merges the two UK notes files: the Skilled Worker, Graduate, Scale-up, Global Business Mobility (GBM) and settlement file (sources [S1] to [S31]) and the talent, self-sponsored and youth-scheme file (sources [S32] to [S60]). Research date 2026-11-14; rules as in force on 2026-11-13. Round 2 is used wherever it conflicts with earlier sections; each place where the earlier reading differed is named in the text. Everything here is a pointer to official sources, not legal advice.

Access grades carry through. "Read" means the official page was opened (some through a text-extraction tool, which the notes flag); "Extract" means a search snippet or title only; "archived" means a web-archive copy. Several GOV.UK pages were refused by the fetch tool as not on the session allowlist; those are recorded as gaps, not as evidence of absence.

Notes to the route table:
- Round 2 changed two route readings. The Global Talent endorsement wait was "usually 5 to 8 weeks" on an undated page [S34] in round 1; the page updated 2026-10-27 gives 3 weeks outside the UK and 8 weeks inside [S55]. Round 2 treats the newer page as governing. The HPI fee was not obtained in round 1 (apply page blocked); round 2 obtained it [S52].
- Round 1 treated the Global Talent English requirement as absent at endorsement. The appendix text gives B1 for GT 13.1(b), rising to B2 for applications on or after 2027-03-26 [S42]. The digital-technology pages state no English requirement at endorsement [S34]. The two may apply to different stages; the notes do not resolve this.

#### Going rates for SOC 2020 codes covering software, IT, data and AI

Source: GOV.UK going rates page, "Updated 22 July 2025" as shown [S1] (grade A, read). Annual salaries are based on a 37.5-hour week; hourly rate in brackets.

| SOC 2020 | Job type (as on the page) | Standard going rate, annual (hourly) | Lower going rate, annual (hourly) | RQF table (Appendix) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2131 | IT project managers | GBP 58,200 (GBP 29.85) | GBP 42,600 (GBP 21.85) | Table 1 (RQF 6 or above) [S2] |
| 2132 | IT managers | GBP 55,000 (GBP 28.21) | GBP 43,000 (GBP 22.05) | Table 1 [S2] |
| 2133 | IT business analysts, architects and systems designers (examples include data architects and data engineers) | GBP 54,900 (GBP 28.15) | GBP 42,400 (GBP 21.74) | Table 1 [S2] |
| 2134 | Programmers and software development professionals | GBP 54,700 (GBP 28.05) | GBP 40,000 (GBP 20.51) | Table 1 [S2] |
| 2135 | Cyber security professionals | GBP 48,500 (GBP 24.87) | GBP 35,300 (GBP 18.10) | Table 1 [S2] |
| 2136 | IT quality and testing professionals | GBP 41,200 (GBP 21.13) | GBP 34,500 (GBP 17.69) | Table 1 [S2] |
| 2137 | IT network professionals | Not extracted (Gap) | Not extracted (Gap) | Table 1 [S2] |
| 2139 | IT professionals n.e.c. (examples include DevOps engineers) | GBP 52,300 (GBP 26.82) | GBP 38,700 (GBP 19.85) | Table 1 [S2] |
| 1137 | Information technology directors | GBP 86,000 (GBP 44.10) | GBP 61,200 (GBP 31.38) | Not checked |
| 3544 | Data analysts | GBP 34,900 (GBP 17.90) | GBP 28,600 (GBP 14.67) | Table 1a (RQF 3 to 5), so not eligible under the RQF 6 rule for Table 1 roles [S2] |
| 2433 | Actuaries, economists and statisticians (includes "statistical data scientists") | GBP 55,100 (GBP 28.26) | GBP 40,700 (GBP 20.87) | Not checked |

- There is no separate AI or machine-learning SOC code on the going-rate page or the Appendix [S1] [S2]. ML and AI roles must be coded to the nearest ICT or statistics code, and the coding choice sets the salary floor.
- The page shows no new-entrant or ISL column. The lower going rate applies only to listed cases (the page cites certain health and education roles, and continuing holders of a Certificate of Sponsorship issued before 2024-04-04) [S1].
- The date shown is "Updated 22 July 2025". The notes record an internal metadata update on 2026-04-29 that is not visible on the page [S1]. The 22 July 2025 date cannot be linked to the GBP 41,700 threshold: no opened page prints the threshold's effective date (see status table).
- Gap: the 2137 and 2433 figures and the ISL status should be confirmed against the live GOV.UK page before use.

#### Comparison and recommendations

| Criterion | Skilled Worker (ICT codes 2131 to 2139) | Graduate route | Global Talent (digital technology) | HPI |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Eligibility | Job at RQF 6 or above (Table 1) with a certificate of sponsorship from a licensed sponsor [S2] [S11] | UK Student visa; completed UK course; provider has reported completion; apply before the Student visa expires [S5] | Recognised leader or potential leader in digital technology in the last 5 years; plus at least 2 of 5 optional criteria; endorsement first [S34] [S35] [S45] | Overseas degree-level qualification awarded in the last 5 years, from a university on the Global Universities List on the award date; age 18 or over [S39] [S38] |
| Salary | Greater of GBP 41,700 and the going rate; discounted rates 70 to 90% of going rate, floor GBP 33,400 [S3] [S4] | None | None | None |
| Time | Visa duration not verified; settlement per the earned-settlement response (10 years default; 5 years at GBP 50,271 or public-service professions; 3 years at GBP 125,141) [S7] | 2 years (applied on or before 2026-12-31) or 18 months (applied on or after 2027-01-01, enacted, not in force) [S5] [S17] | Endorsement "3 weeks" outside the UK or "8 weeks" inside (page updated 2026-10-27) [S55]; visa "usually" 3 or 8 weeks [S37]; up to 5 years per grant [S42] | 2 years; 3 years for a doctorate [S39] |
| Cost (government, as shown) | Visa GBP 819 (outside UK, up to 3 years) or GBP 943 (inside UK); IHS per year not verified; sponsor licence and CoS paid by the employer [S11] | GBP 937 application fee; IHS GBP 1,035 per year (GBP 1,552.50 for 18 months) [S5] (archived) | GBP 561 at endorsement plus GBP 205 at visa = GBP 766; IHS "usually" GBP 1,035 per year [S33] [S37] | GBP 880 application fee; IHS "usually" GBP 1,035 per year (GBP 2,070 for 2 years) [S52] |
| Employer dependence | High: licensed sponsor and CoS | Low: no sponsor | None | None |
| Settlement path | Counts towards settlement under the earned-settlement response; B2 English for settlement from 2027-03-26 (enacted) [S7] [S8] | Does not count towards settlement [S7] | ILR after 3 years (exceptional talent) or 5 years (exceptional promise) [S42] | Not a route to settlement [S39] |

Round 2 changed two readings used in this table: the Global Talent endorsement wait (3 and 8 weeks, not 5 to 8 weeks), and the settlement timing (the response replaces the round 1 expectation of a December 2026 Statement of Changes, which was not found as published).

Recommendations (one per profile; failure mode per recommended route):

| Profile | Recommended route | Failure mode |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Mid-level engineer with an offer | Skilled Worker, coded to the nearest ICT SOC code (for example 2134 or 2139). Check that the offer meets the greater of GBP 41,700 and the SOC going rate (2134 standard GBP 54,700; 2139 standard GBP 52,300) [S1] [S3] | The offer is below the going rate for the chosen code, or the role is coded to a Table 1a code (for example 3544, RQF 3 to 5), which does not meet the RQF 6 requirement [S2] |
| (b) Senior engineer or researcher without an offer | Global Talent (digital technology) for the engineer; Global Talent (researcher or academic leader) if the work is research. Skilled Worker is not a route without an offer (round 1) [S36] | Endorsement is refused on the evidence: the cap is 10 documents of up to 3 A4 sides (CV and letters outside the cap), with 3 letters from different authors each known to the applicant for at least 12 months [S43]. Refund of the GBP 561 endorsement fee if the endorsement is refused: not checked (Gap) |
| (c) Recent graduate | Graduate route, if the applicant holds or has held a UK Student visa and completed a UK course: apply before 2026-12-31 for 2 years, or from 2027-01-01 for 18 months. Then Skilled Worker if an offer is made [S5] [S17]. Where the degree is overseas, HPI is the alternative [S39] | Graduate: the route cannot be extended and does not count towards settlement [S5] [S7]. HPI: the university is not on the Global Universities List on the award date, or the degree is older than 5 years; HPI is not a route to settlement [S39] [S38] |

Objections recorded in the notes: the threshold effective date is unresolved (22 July 2025 and April 2026 are both unconfirmed); the going-rate table has no AI-specific code, so the coding choice moves the salary floor; the earned-settlement response is not yet a Statement of Changes and final terms may differ [S6]; fee figures for Skilled Worker and sponsor rest on a law-firm summary (B).

#### Gaps for this country

- Skilled Worker: processing times and service standards (GOV.UK processing-times page refused by the fetch tool); dependants; the effective date of the GBP 41,700 threshold; the Immigration Health Surcharge per year for Skilled Worker; the Immigration Skills Charge; the GOV.UK fee table.
- Temporary Shortage List: content, government decision, expiry and occupation list; whether it replaced the Immigration Salary List.
- Scale-up: thresholds, duration, settlement path (title only seen). GBM: thresholds and durations (appendix seen in search only). CETA Service Supplier visa: cap and term (title only).
- Graduate: processing time; capture date of the archived page unresolved (between 2026-08-13 and 2026-11-03).
- Global Talent: endorsing body not named on the 2026 re-read; endorsement fee and decision time for the AI-research bodies; refund policy; fee effective date; English requirement at endorsement versus visa; endorsement pass rates (no official publication found; the 63% figure traces to a commercial agency, graded E).
- HPI: fee effective date and dependant fee; earlier lists (top 40, widening to top 100) not seen.
- Innovator Founder: fee page blocked on re-read; endorsing bodies not named; IHS not stated.
- YPS: 2025 and 2027 places; number selected in 2026; savings holding period; TB, documents and consular waits for applicants.
- Settlement: implementing Statement of Changes not found; consultation dates unverified; Home Office settlement guidance version (v19.0) not located.
- Youth Mobility Scheme (general): not researched. Remote work: foreign-employer and contractor position for Skilled Worker and Graduate not checked; HMRC residence test not opened.
- Indian nationals: TB clinic list; visa-centre waits; document attestation; police clearance; degree recognition; Home Office nationality statistics.

### Ireland

Scope: employer-sponsored permits (CSEP, GEP, ICT, Contract for Services, Reactivation, Dependant/Partner/Spouse), Stamp 4 as the path to residence, Stamp 1G, the thresholds and the occupation list, India-specific items, and the routes that were not researched. Rules are reported as in force on 2026-11-13 (round 1) with round-2 additions retrieved 2026-11-14 (round 2). Where round 2 differs from round 1, round 2 is used and the earlier reading is named in the text. Grades and read or extract status are copied from the notes; none has been raised.

#### Route table

| Route | Kind | Eligibility | Salary threshold and date | Processing | Fees | Dependants | Path to permanent residence | Source | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Critical Skills Employment Permit (CSEP) | Employer-sponsored, skilled | Relevant degree or higher; occupation on the Critical Skills Occupations List (CSOL, S.I. 213 of 2026, effective 2026-05-13); job offer of at least 2 years [S70]. The Department labels the two salary tiers "with relevant degree" and "without relevant degree" [S61]; S70 states that occupations above EUR 68,911 a year are also eligible, so the official framings differ and the eligibility text should be checked before acting (contested, see Status table). Public-sector pay-deal roles with a relevant degree are exempt from the CSEP minimum from 2026 [S61] | EUR 40,904 a year with relevant degree (CSOL occupations, from 2026-03-01); EUR 36,848 a year for a graduate within 12 months of qualifying; EUR 68,911 a year in the second tier (S61 label "without relevant degree") [S61, S70] | No service standard. Queue position on 2026-11-12: application receipt date 2026-10-23 [S65]. This is a receipt date, not a processing time | EUR 1,000 first application, up to 24 months (fee table dated 2021-03-22) [S68, S70] | Immediate family reunification; the Dependant/Partner/Spouse Employment Permit has no fee [S70, S68] | Stamp 4 after 21 months from commencement of employment (categories i to iii) [S67]; the 21-month period is stated as Department of Justice and Immigration Service page text; its legal basis was not opened (Gap) | [S61, S64, S65, S67, S68, S70] | A |

S76 describes the 50:50 count as EEA, UK or Swiss nationals [S76] (conflict, not reconciled in the notes). Discrepancies logged in the route table:
- CSEP tiers: the Department's wording (S61) and S70 (S70) describe the two-tier structure differently. Not resolved.
- Stamp 4 timing for GEP holders: 57 months (S67) against 5 years (S69). Not resolved.
- Stamp 4 timing for CSEP: round 1 prior P4 said 2 years; S67 says 21 months. The 21-month figure is used.
- 50:50 rule: S69 says EEA nationals, S76 says EEA, UK or Swiss nationals. Not reconciled in the notes.
- Fee table S68 is dated 2021-03-22 and applies to permits received on or after 2014-10-01; no 2025 or 2026 fee change appears on S63. Staleness risk.
- Round 2 adds ICT and Contract for Services thresholds (S76, grade B), which round 1 recorded as a Gap, and the Stamp 1G text (S77), which round 1 did not research.

#### Employer reality

Official data
- Permit statistics for 2025 are published as xlsx tables (nationality, company, county, sector) [S71, S75]. The landing page carries no figures and the files were not readable by WebFetch, so the 2024, 2025 and 2026 year-to-date counts, the India share and the ICT sector share are Gap.
- A newspaper headline (grade C, not used for figures) says permits issued in 2025 "drop by almost a quarter" against the previous year [S72].
- No official measure of how often employers sponsor CSEP or GEP for AI, ML or data roles was found. Gap.

Employer cost and effort
- Government fee: EUR 1,000 for a first application up to 24 months [S68, S70]. The payer is not stated on the pages read. 90% refund on refusal, as stated on the CSEP page [S70].
- GEP: LMNT required in most cases, with the advertising steps in the route table; 50:50 rule as in the route table [S69].
- CSEP: offers must be at least 2 years in duration [S70].
- Stamp 4 applications go to the Department of Justice; no DETE support letter since 2023-11-30 [S70].

Offer-to-start timeline
- No service standard found. The queue positions on 2026-11-12 are in the route table [S65].
- M (derived from S65): the queue was about three weeks behind receipt for CSEP and about four weeks for GEP. This is queue lag, not elapsed processing time.
- Offer-to-start time for an actual hire: Gap. No employer or law-firm survey was opened.

Backlog signal
- Stamp 4 interim measure for CSEP holders who applied before expiry, ended 2026-08-31 "while the Immigration Service Delivery backlog is cleared" [S63].
- Search snippets mention Stamp 4 and IRP card renewal delays and a Dail statement that permit delays are "crippling the economy" (undated, not opened; grade C or E). Not used.

Sponsorship for AI or GenAI roles: not measured. Gap.

#### Indian nationals

- Caps, quotas and bilateral schemes: none found on pages read in round 1 [S67 to S71]. No full India search was run. Gap.
- Embassy processing, New Delhi (round 2, Read, grade A) [S74]: employment visa Category A, first decision 4 to 6 weeks; Category B, 8 to 10 weeks; appeal 6 calendar months; study first decision 4 to 8 weeks; business, conference and event 4 weeks; visit 6 to 8 weeks; join family first decision 12 months, appeal 12 calendar months. The page states times in working days from receipt, excluding weekends and closure dates, and describes them as not binding. The table uses weeks and months and the unit is inconsistent as printed. The page does not map "Employment, Category A/B" to CSEP or GEP. The round-1 reading (DFA page redirected to ireland.ie, not followed) is superseded by this page on api.ireland.ie.
- Permit queue vs Embassy: these times cover Embassy-issued visas. CSEP and GEP permits are issued by DETE and the Immigration Service, so these times do not measure the permit queue [S65, S74].
- Documents: the page does not list documents for Indian applicants; it refers to a separate "Visas and Documentation" page, not opened. It states biometric clearance is required before a decision; DigiLocker documents are given as an example of easily verifiable documents for applicants resident in India; the fee payment confirmation e-mail must be brought to the Visa Application Centre from 2026-11-16; flight bookings are not required [S74].
- Apostille, police certificate and TB test for India: Gap. Not stated on the page read; not in any source read.
- Degree or qualification recognition for Indian nationals, relevant to the CSEP "relevant degree" test: Gap. Search returned consultancy and forum pages on NARIC recognition, not opened (grade C or E), and Oireachtas questions, not opened. The recognising body is not confirmed.
- Good character test for Stamp 4 applies to all applicants with no Indian-specific term found [S70]. Stamp 4 "good character" and document rules for Indian applicants: not searched.
- Indian share of permits: Gap (see Employer reality).
- Spouse or dependant work rights for Indian families: Gap for the rights text (see route table).
- Plainly: nothing found that treats Indian nationals differently in the permit rules read; nothing was searched for on the documents, recognition or medical side beyond the points above.

#### Remote work while resident

- Per main route: CSEP and GEP are issued against a named job offer [S67, S70] (inference, M). Neither page read states whether a holder may work for a foreign employer or as a contractor. Gap for CSEP, GEP, Stamp 4 and Stamp 1G, which are not covered by any page read on this point. Stamp 1G holders may work full time, not self-employed or operating a business, for an employer [S77]; this is the only route text read that addresses work type.
- Tax residence: Revenue (www.revenue.ie) is the official source; not opened. No tax advice given.

#### Comparison and recommendations

| Route | Eligibility | Time (official) | Cost | Employer dependence | Settlement path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CSEP | Relevant degree; CSOL occupation (SOC 2136 programmers and software development professionals; 2137; 2139; 2423 and 2425 only with specialisms); salary EUR 40,904 a year with degree (CSOL) or EUR 68,911 a year in the second tier (S61 label); offer of at least 2 years [S61, S64, S70] | Queue position: receipt date 2026-10-23 on 2026-11-12; no service standard [S65] | EUR 1,000 first application [S68, S70] | High: the employer applies against an offer | Stamp 4 after 21 months [S67] |
| GEP | Most occupations (ineligible list not reproduced); LMNT; 50:50 rule (see conflict); quotas for some roles; EUR 36,605 a year standard; EUR 34,009 a year Irish Level 8+ graduate [S61, S69] | Queue position: receipt date 2026-10-13 on 2026-11-12 [S65] | EUR 1,000 first application [S68] | High: employer applies; LMNT advert; 50:50 rule | Stamp 4 after 57 months [S67] or 5 years [S69]: unresolved |
| Stamp 1G (graduates of Irish programmes, Stamp 2 holders) | NFQ Level 8+ on the ILEP; notified on or after 2017-01-01; apply within six months of notification; Stamp 2 and registration card held; seven-year student limit [S77] | Duration up to 12 months (Level 8) or 24 months (Level 9 and above) [S77] | Not recorded in notes (Gap) | Lower for employment permits, since holders are exempt from needing one, under a policy that "could change" [S77] | Not stated in the document (Gap) |

Recommendations
1. Mid-level engineer with an offer: apply for CSEP if the job maps to a CSOL entry (most likely SOC 2136) and pay meets EUR 40,904 a year with a relevant degree, or EUR 68,911 a year in the second tier as the Department labels it [S61, S64]. Failure mode: the title does not map to a CSOL entry ("data scientist", "AI engineer" and "ML engineer" are not named [S64]), so the employer falls back to GEP and faces an LMNT, the 50:50 rule and quota closure risk [S69, S63].
2. Senior engineer or researcher without an offer: no job-seeker route was found (Gap). Both CSEP and GEP are issued against an employer offer [S70, S67] (M). Failure mode: no sponsor and no route on the pages read for a person without an offer. Do not plan a move on the assumption of a route that has not been checked.
3. Recent graduate: CSEP graduate threshold EUR 36,848 a year applies if the qualification was received within 12 months before application and the role is on CSOL [S61, S70]; GEP Irish Level 8+ graduate threshold EUR 34,009 a year [S69]. Where the graduate studied in Ireland on a Stamp 2, Stamp 1G is a route to check: it is a round 2 finding from a dated 2017 revision [S77], not a round-1 reading. Failure mode: the 12-month window lapses before an offer is made, the role is off-list, or the six-month Stamp 1G application window lapses.

Round 2 note: round 1 recorded Stamp 1G as not researched; round 2 (S77) supplies the text, graded A but dated, so the Stamp 1G row above is a later and better-checked reading.

### Germany

Scope: Germany routes for skilled tech and AI work. Sources A to L were retrieved 2026-11-13 (notes header). Sources first defined in round 2 (S94 onward) are dated 2026-11-14 in the writing plan, while the notes' round 2 header gives 2026-11-13 as the round date; this report uses the plan's dates for retrieval and the notes' dates for the status of each fact. Where round 2 conflicts with round 1, round 2 is used and the earlier reading is named in the text. Statute text for §18g and §18c could not be read (gesetze-im-internet.de blocked, [S82], [S83]); the §18g floors rest on the BMI notice [S94] (Read, as cited). Pointers, not legal advice.

#### Employer reality

- Blue Card volume: more than 41,000 EU Blue Cards were first issued in 2023, up 5% on 2022; about 113,500 Blue Card holders lived in Germany at end 2023 [S90] (BAMF, Read, last updated 2024-07-04). No 2024 or 2025 figures and no occupation breakdown on that page.
- Sponsorship for AI and software roles: not evidenced. No official register by occupation was found.
- Employer obligations and cost: BA approval is needed unless the salary is at or above 50,700 EUR [S84]; a foreign degree must be recognised [S84]. The fast-track fee is an employer-side agreement fee in the Munich range (411 EUR shown; payer implied, not explicit) [S88].
- Offer-to-start timeline: fast-track steps 2 to 4 weeks plus a visa decision within 3 weeks of a complete application [S88]. End-to-end timing is not published and has not been measured for Indian applicants.
- Opportunity Card visas issued: not found; not searched for numbers beyond snippets [S101].

#### Indian nationals

- Share of Blue Cards: more than one-quarter of 2023 first issues went to Indian nationals; the page gives no count [S90] (BAMF, A, Read, 2024-07-04). The 2024 and 2025 share was not found on any official page opened.
- The 90,000 arrangement: media report only (2024-10-25; C) [S93]; the jobbatical page (C, 2026-10-14) gives no number and no official source [S96]. An official joint declaration or BMI or Bundesregierung source was not found. Treat as unconfirmed.
- Consular channels (A, Read) [S95]: New Delhi consular services by prior appointment only, through a booking form; Mumbai visa section open Monday to Friday by appointment allocation only; visa enquiries by email (visa@mumb.diplo.de), with no phone for visa status; Bangalore visa hotline Monday to Thursday 14:00 to 15:30 (+91 80 45 56 01 11). The page gives no waiting times, and it is undated in the notes. Waiting time in India: not found on an official page. The New Delhi consulate page [S91] (A, Read, undated) also gives no waiting times.
- Document verification (A, Read, leaflet valid from 06/2026) [S97]: the verification procedure at New Delhi has been digitised since March 2026. Apostille does not apply between Germany and India (India joined the Hague Convention, Germany objected). Legalisation was discontinued in 2000. Verification checks formal genuineness and substance, carried out by paid trusted lawyers or service providers; the embassy issues an assessment, and the sticker confirms only that a check was made. Hindi, Punjabi or Urdu documents need a simple translation into English; sworn and German translations are not required; English documents need none. Simple copies suffice; scans in PDF, colour, maximum 15 MB per email. Processing time 'ca. 8 Wochen' (a processing time, not a waiting time; standard or median not stated). Flat fees EUR 400 (Delhi, Haryana, Punjab, Rajasthan and others) or EUR 500 (Jammu and Kashmir, Ladakh, island territories). Scope: the leaflet is addressed to German authorities and covers birth, marriage, divorce and identity documents.
- Police clearance: not required according to the same leaflet. No other official source on police clearance for Indian applicants was found; the applicant-side position is a gap.
- Degree recognition for Indian degrees: the federal portal [S99] confirms only that it covered professional and university degrees from India and that anabin was updated (no detail). Rules for Indian degrees are not verified in detail; anabin must be checked per institution (not opened). ZAB is named only as co-host.
- The IT-without-degree route (2 years in 5, 45,630 EUR, BA approval) is listed on the federal graphic as relevant to Indian applicants [S85]. Its settlement path is not verified.
- Medical and TB requirements: not searched.

#### Remote work while resident

- Per route (Blue Card, Opportunity Card, skilled-worker titles): not searched. Whether a holder may work for a foreign employer or as a contractor while resident in Germany is not verified for any route.
- The Opportunity Card permits side work of up to 20 hours a week and trial employment of up to 2 weeks per employer [S87]. This concerns work in Germany and is not a remote-work rule.
- Tax-residence pointer: not verified; the notes record no official tax source. Not given here.

#### Comparison and recommendations

| Route | Eligibility | Time | Cost (as in notes) | Employer dependence | Settlement path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EU Blue Card, §18g (standard 50,700 or reduced 45,934.20 EUR per year, 2026) | Recognised foreign degree; reduced floor for bottleneck professions; graduate rule contested [S84], [S86], [S94] | Fast-track 2 to 4 weeks plus a visa decision within 3 weeks of a complete application (Munich, one authority) [S88] | Fast-track 411 EUR (range) and visa fee 75 EUR shown; recognition fees where applicable [S88] | High: BA approval below 50,700 EUR [S84] | Niederlassungserlaubnis after 27 months with A1, or 21 with B1 [S89] |
| IT without degree (3 years in 7 at 45,934.20 EUR; or 2 years in 5 at 45,630 EUR, 2026) | IT experience as stated; BA approval required [S85] | Not in notes | Not in notes | High: BA approval required; salary minimum does not apply under collective agreements [S85] | Not verified for this route |
| Opportunity Card (§20a AufenthG; points, minimum 6) | Qualification or training plus A1 German or B2 English and at least 6 points [S87] | Job search up to 1 year; extension up to 2 years on a concrete job offer [S87] | Blocked funds of 1,091 EUR net per month (2026) or a commitment; fee not found [S87] | Low for the search period; extension needs a concrete job offer [S87] | Not in notes |

Reader recommendations (not tested against employer data; round 1 of the notes):
- (a) Mid-level engineer with an offer: EU Blue Card, or the IT route where it fits, with an employer-led fast-track procedure under §81a. Failure mode: salary below the threshold once the notes' excluded items are removed, or degree recognition not completed before the visa application.
- (b) Senior engineer or researcher without an offer: Opportunity Card for the job search (points route, up to 1 year). Failure mode: fewer than 6 points, or the 1,091 EUR per month funds not blocked.
- (c) Graduate within three years of graduation: EU Blue Card at the reduced floor. Failure mode: the graduate reduced rule is not in the official text read (S84 gives no figure; S86 reads 45.3%); verify before relying on either figure.

Approval odds are not estimated.

#### Gaps for this country

- Statute text: §18g and §18c (blocked, not retried); §19c(2) AufenthG, §20a, §81a and §6 BeschV not read.
- 2025 Blue Card floors: no official source (hypothesis only).
- 2026 contribution ceiling on an official page: not opened (law-firm B only).
- 2027 Blue Card floors: not published as of 2026-11-13; publication timing not verifiable.
- Graduate reduced rule figure: contested and unresolved.
- Consular waiting times in India: not found on any official page.
- Police clearance and medical or TB rules for Indian applicants: not found, or not searched.
- Blue Card issuance for 2024 and 2025, and the Indian share: not found.
- Opportunity Card issue numbers, fee, start date and planned changes: not verified.
- 90,000 arrangement: no official source.
- Anabin per institution and ZAB procedure: not checked.
- Work-and-Stay-Agentur: no bill, date or launch found beyond search snippets.
- Settlement for the IT routes: not verified.
- Over-45 threshold: route attachment not stated; single source.
- End-to-end fast-track time (K5 '2 to 3 months'): unverifiable.
- Not researched: §18a, §18b, ICT card, research title, freelance title, job-seeker titles other than the Opportunity Card, founder route, remote-work title; dependants for all routes; processing for most routes; remote work per route; tax residence; naturalisation after the 2025 change; employer sponsorship counts for AI or software roles.
- Pages not opened or blocked: gesetze-im-internet.de (§18g, §18c, refused); the Auswärtiges Amt visa-operator page for India (not found); the anabin and ZAB guidance pages (listed in search, not opened).

### Netherlands

Scope: work-authorisation routes for skilled tech and AI workers, rules as in force on 2026-11-13 (anchor 2026-11-11). Sources S102 to S109 were retrieved 2026-11-13 per the notes. Sources S110 to S114 were first defined in round 2, which the writing plan dates 2026-11-14; the notes give 2026-11-13 for them, so the notes' dates are kept here and the difference is left for the coordinator.

Grades are copied from the notes and not raised. Almost every A in this part was read through a summarising fetch tool (Read via WebFetch summary). The writing plan caps summary-only figures at B until the primary page is re-read; that cap is not applied here.

Where round 2 conflicts with round 1, round 2 is used and the earlier reading is recorded in the text:
- 30%-regeling status: the earlier note said enacted (S103). Round 2 records a conflict with the Ministry of Finance page (S110), so the 2027 rate and threshold are V0.
- Naturalisation: the earlier note cited a 2-month consultation and a closed consultation status. The draft memorandum (S111) shows the consultation phase as not set and the bill as not yet submitted.
- MVV for India: the earlier note treated the MVV as likely required (D). The IND-linked page (S112) confirms it applies where the IND letter says the sticker can be requested in India.
- Zoekjaar ranking test: the earlier hypothesis named QS, THE and ARWU. Round 2 records top 200 in at least 2 of THE, QS and ShanghaiRanking (ARWU and GRAS by subject).
- Norms: re-verified on S113 and S114, which agree with S102.

Round 1 cited processing periods to a source number that does not match the ledger entry for S111, so those periods (statutory 90 days for kennismigrant decisions, 30 days for recognised-sponsor Blue Card applications, 90 days plus 30 for zoekjaar) have no valid source. They are graded D and are not used in the tables below. The Blue Card periods of 45 and 90 days come from the page recorded as S106 and are used. Two other round 1 citations (for the naturalisation proposal and a media report) likewise match no ledger row; the media report is D and unsourced.

#### Comparison and recommendations

| Route | Eligibility | Time | Cost | Employer dependence | Settlement path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HSM via recognised sponsor | Employer is a recognised sponsor; salary EUR 5,942.00 (30 or older), 4,357.00 (under 30), 3,122.00 (reduced), gross per month [S102] | Recognition up to 3 months once [S107]; decision period not confirmed (gap) | IND fee not found (gap); recognition fee depends on headcount, amount not found (gap) | High: employer must be recognised | Permanent residence after 5 years not checked (gap) |
| EU Blue Card | Degree (3 years or more, Nuffic-valued) or 5 years experience; contract at least 6 months; EUR 5,942.00 standard or 4,754.00 reduced [S106] | 45 or 90 days per S106 page [S106] | Not found (gap) | Medium: no recognised sponsor needed [S106] | EU long-term residence possible after 2 years in some situations [S106] |
| Zoekjaar hoogopgeleiden | Qualifying NL education or research, or a designated foreign institution; Nuffic-valued diploma; IELTS 6.0 [S104] | 1-year permit; processing not stated (gap) | Fee not found (gap) | None to start; switch to HSM or Blue Card on an offer [S104] | Cannot be extended [S104] |

- (a) Mid-level engineer with an offer: use the Blue Card if the employer is not a recognised sponsor; use HSM if it is. Failure mode: salary below EUR 5,942.00 gross per month at age 30 or older (holiday allowance excluded), or the employer lacks recognition.
- (b) Senior engineer or researcher without an offer: no job-seeker route found in scope (gap). Zoekjaar applies only to graduates or to research in NL within the 3 years before [S104]. Failure mode: no route exists until an offer is in hand.
- (c) Graduate of a qualifying programme: zoekjaar hoogopgeleiden (1 year, free to work) [S104], then switch to HSM at the reduced criterion, EUR 3,122.00 gross per month [S102]. Failure mode: foreign university not in the top 200 in at least 2 of the 3 rankings, or Nuffic valuation not completed.

### France

Scope: France only, rules as in force on the research date. Round-2 sources are dated 2026-11-14 by the research plan (the ledger entries for the same sources say 2026-11-13); this section uses the plan date. No French primary text was opened as a page of record. The Légifrance decree texts [S117] and the France-Visas talent page [S119] were blocked, and the Ministry shortage-list and statistics pages [S124][S125] failed. Every threshold below is therefore a secondary source or a summary of an official page.

Grades: several official pages were read only through a summarising fetch tool ([S127], [S131], [S132], [S133], [S134]). The research notes graded some of them A; under the summary rule they are capped at B here. No figure in this section is grade A. Read and Extract status is given in each source cell.

Round 2 supersedes round 1 where they conflict:
- CBE minimum contract length: round 1 gave six months [S116][S120] (verdict updated). Round 2 reads no minimum as an eligibility condition [S127], so the six-month figure is contested (verdict differs).
- CBE salary floor of 59 373 EUR: round 1 graded it B from commentary; round 2 confirms it from a summary of the official site [S134], still graded B.
- Language: round 1 said Talent holders were exempt from the language rules [S122]. Round 2 does not support that for the carte de résident [S133]. Round 1 gave no level for the multi-year card; round 2 gives A2 [S133].
- Intra-company transfers: the "1.0 times average salary" wording in decree 2026-1001 applies to ICT permits, not to the CBE, which is 1.5 times [S127][S128].

Unresolved discrepancies: the Talent reference salary instrument (arrêté of 21 August 2025 [S121]; publication on 31 August 2025 [S118]; arrêté of 3 May 2025 [S116], unconfirmed). The decree 2026-308 date (24 April 2026 [S116]; 25 April 2026 [S115]). The language-level instrument (arrêté of 22 July 2025 [S122]; arrêté of 22 December 2025 cited as the proof reference [S133]).

Dates: the research plan dates the round-2 sources 2026-11-14; the ledger dates them 2026-11-13. Undated pages are recorded as of 2026-11-14 (retrieval date).

#### Employer reality

- Statistics: no official count of Talent Passports by category or nationality was found. The Ministry statistics page failed [S125]. A search on Talent Passport statistics returned only Indian passport-issuance news, which is not usable. Number and year: not available.
- Sponsorship for AI and software roles: not found in any source opened.
- Employer cost and effort: commentary says the Talent route is faster than a classic work authorisation, with no figures [S121] (C). The only fee found is the 350 EUR grant fee [S134] (B).
- Offer-to-start timeline: no official processing time or service standard was found for any Talent category. The only figures are prefecture decision deadlines [S134]: implicit refusal after 4 months for most categories, 90 days for the CBE, 60 days for the chercheur. These are not service standards or historical medians, and no historical median was found.

#### Indian nationals

- Caps or backlogs: none found in sources opened.
- Bilateral scheme: no France-India work-mobility measure found. An Élysée page dated 18 Feb 2026 and a press snippet report a target of 30 000 Indian students a year by 2030. The snippet is a student measure only and was not opened [S135] (E for work, C/E for the student target).
- Visa-centre operator and waits: not established from an official page. The France-Visas India page appeared in search but was not opened. A secondary snippet reports service interruptions; its dates were not read [S119][S135]. No dated official wait was found.
- Document attestation: foreign public acts need legalisation. The procedure is double legalisation, with a French translation by a traducteur habilité before the legalisation steps. The page does not mention India or the Hague Convention. Read via summary of the official justice.fr page [S132] (capped at B). India's apostille status was not verified in any source opened.
- Police certificate: not researched.
- Degree recognition: an agreement signed 10 March 2018 says French authorities accept AISSCE, Bachelor's, Master's and PhD degrees from government-approved Indian institutions. Professional degrees regulated by a professional council are excluded. The agreement is study-oriented; the page does not map Indian degrees to French levels and gives no entry-into-force date. Its use in a work-permit file is not stated. Read via summary of the Campus France India page [S131] (capped at B). Comparability for employment (Ciep): not found.
- Indian share of Talent Passports: not found.
- TB or medical rules: not covered by these sources.
- Other differences: none found in sources opened.

#### Remote work while resident

- Salarié en mission: the holder cannot change employer and keep the card [S121] (C).
- Talent holders working for a foreign employer or as a contractor: no statement in any source opened [S127][S129][S133][S134]. Not established.
- Tax residence: no pointer to an official source was established. Not researched.

#### Comparison and recommendations

| Route | Eligibility | Decision deadline | Cost | Employer dependence | Settlement path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EU Blue Card (talent-carte bleue européenne) | Offer; degree (3 years) or 5 years' experience; gross annual salary at least 59 373 EUR [S121][S134] | 90 days (30 days if CBE holder in another EU state), implicit refusal [S134] | 350 EUR on grant [S134] | High: salary floor and contract at filing | Carte de résident needs B1 [S133]; long-term EU card language rule after 5 years [S133]. Years not verified |
| Salarié qualifié | Offer; Master-level diploma; gross annual salary at least 39 582 EUR [S121][S134] | 4 months, implicit refusal for most categories [S134] | 350 EUR on grant [S134] | High: salary floor and employer contract | As above. Years not verified |
| Chercheur (researcher) | Host agreement; minimum 2 200 EUR gross, period not stated [S134] | 60 days, implicit refusal [S134] | 350 EUR on grant [S134] | High: host agreement | As above. Years not verified |

- (a) Mid-level engineer with an offer: CBE if the offer meets 59 373 EUR gross annual and the diploma or experience condition; otherwise salarié qualifié if Master-level and at least 39 582 EUR gross annual. Failure mode: salary below the floor on the filing date. Commentary advises checking the threshold in force on the filing date [S121].
- (b) Senior engineer or researcher without an offer: chercheur, but only with a host agreement [S134]. Failure mode: no host agreement, so the route is closed. No other route without an offer was verified; the content of decree 2026-308 is unverified [S116].
- (c) Recent graduate: salarié qualifié only if an offer reaches 39 582 EUR gross annual and a Master-level diploma applies [S121][S134]. Failure mode: salary below 39 582 EUR gross annual. No graduate route was verified.

- Shortage list and ICT or AI occupations: blocked [S124].

- Service standards or historical medians for any talent category: not found; only prefecture decision deadlines [S134].
- Quotas for Talent categories: none stated by commentary [S121]; not verified against official text.
- Innovative-company employee path: eligibility not extracted.
- Talent categories after "profession artistique et culturelle": the summary [S134] was truncated; the remaining categories were not seen.
- Graduate APS and talent jeune diplômé: not found.
- French Tech Visa: no official page opened.
- Remote work and freelancing for Talent holders: no official statement found; tax-residence pointer not established.
- Settlement years to carte de résident and naturalisation: not verified.
- Indian visa-centre operator and waits (dated); police certificate; TB or medical rules; comparability (Ciep) for employment; India's apostille status: not established.
- Long-stay visa consular fee: not tested.
- Talent route proposals for 2026 and settlement reform: not researched.

### Switzerland

Source basis: notes round 1 (retrieved 2026-11-13) and round 2 (retrieved 2026-11-14). Where they conflict, round 2 is used, and the text says so. Conflicts: (1) Graduate exemption: round 1 gave a 'from 2025' date that no read source supports; round 2 suggests adoption in 2022 from headlines only, which is not verified. (2) 2027 quotas: round 1 cited a consultation draft known only by filename and blocked [S141]; round 2 cites stakeholder statements dated 2026-06-29 and 2026-07-14, seen in search results only [S150]. Neither round found a decision. (3) Article numbers: the brief's Art. 23 reference and the Art. 18 AIG/LEI reference in round 1 are both unconfirmed, because the statute page was refused.

Grades and status are copied from the notes: A (primary official page read), B (snippet or extract of an official page), C (secondary source), D (unverified), Gap (not verified), E (absence). Read means the page was opened; Extract means a search snippet only.

JSONL mapping: routes with no source read (job-search permit, researchers, intra-company transfers, start-up, points-based, remote work, EU Blue Card, settlement) are absence records in the JSONL, not visa_route records. The EFTA–India row and the 2027 quota are rule records, because the kind field has no value for them.

#### Employer reality

- Statistics: no SEM statistics by canton, occupation or nationality were found. The SEM employment-series PDFs seen in search are Ukraine-related and carry no such breakdown [S151] (Gap).
- Sponsorship for AI and software roles: no figure found (Gap).
- Cap: the 2026 federal quotas cap the route at 8,500 in total [S136, A]. A third-country hire needs a quota allocation through the cantonal procedure. The cantonal steps are on a separate procedure page referred to in [S145], which was not read.
- Priority test: an employer must show no suitable candidate on the domestic market or in the EU/EFTA labour markets [S137, A].
- Employer effort: positions in listed occupations must be reported to the RAV where the national unemployment rate reaches or exceeds 5%; a EURES posting is also required; employers must document search efforts [S145, A, Read; page last change 21.12.2020].
- Job-posting notification deadline and sanction: not stated on the page read [S143] (Gap).
- Employer cost and offer-to-start timeline: not found (Gap).

#### Indian nationals

- Caps and backlogs: the 2026 quotas are stated for third-country nationals generally [S136, A]. No India-specific cap or backlog was found in the sources read. Not separately searched.
- Bilateral scheme: the EFTA–India agreement entered into force on 2025-10-01 [S144, A]. A secondary report says Switzerland gives visas to independent professionals and visas for spouses of intra-corporate transferees [S148, C]. Treaty text not read; the Indian government's annex 8A PDF [S149] was found in search and not opened (B at most, extract).
- Visa centre and waits: a one-pager names VFS Global as the operator [S146, B, extract]; no waiting time was seen. The EDA India page redirected and the redirect was not followed [S147] (Gap). No dated waiting time was found.
- Visa D: no dated processing figure found (Gap).
- Document attestation, apostille, police certificate, Indian degree recognition, TB or medical rules: not searched in rounds 1 or 2. Nothing found.
- SEM permit statistics for Indian nationals: none found; the statistics series seen in search has no Indian breakdown [S151] (Gap).

#### Remote work while resident

- Not verified. No official source was read on whether a B or L holder may work for a foreign employer or as a contractor.
- Tax residence: no official pointer was read; the notes name no official tax source.

#### Comparison and recommendations

Only the federal quota route (L and B permits) is documented on official pages. The comparison below is limited by the gaps: time, cost and settlement path were not read.

| Route | Eligibility | Time | Cost | Employer dependence | Settlement path |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L permit | Qualified worker; priority and salary-norm tests [S137] | Not read | Not read | Employer-sponsored; quota 4,000 and priority test [S136] | Not read |
| B permit | As L permit; integration criteria for multi-year stay [S145] | Not read | Not read | Employer-sponsored; quota 4,500 [S136] | Multi-year; B-to-C route not read |
| Graduate exemption | Swiss-university Master's and PhD (per 2021 release); in force not verified [S142] | Not read | Not read | Not read | Not read |
| EFTA–India agreement | Natural persons annex; Swiss commitments not read [S149] | Not read | Not read | Not read | Not read |

One-line recommendations (no approval odds are estimated):

- (a) Mid-level engineer with an offer: an L or B permit, subject to the federal quota and the priority check. Failure mode: quota exhaustion, or a cantonal refusal on the priority or salary test.
- (b) Senior engineer or researcher without an offer: no job-search route was verified for this reader. Failure mode: no route found in this session.
- (c) Recent graduate: the Swiss-degree job-search permit was not verified; the quota-exempt Master's and PhD route was found only at consultation stage. Failure mode: the exemption date and conditions are unverified.

## Open Questions & Risks

**Confidence: medium.** The rule text and figures for the UK Skilled Worker threshold, the German Blue Card floors, the Irish CSEP and GEP thresholds, the French Blue Card salary and the Dutch norms were read on official pages or on official summaries, and most were re-read in round 2 with consistent results. Confidence is held below high because: (1) no statute text was read for Germany, France or Switzerland, and the UK Immigration Rules were read only through summaries; (2) the UK earned-settlement response is policy, not yet an instrument; (3) the Dutch 30% ruling status and the Irish Stamp 4 legal basis are unresolved; (4) processing times and fees are missing or summary-only for most routes; (5) no permit counts by nationality or occupation were extracted, so the "how likely is an employer to use it" question is answered by eligibility, not by observed use; (6) the Indian-national items (waits, documents, police clearance, degree recognition) are largely gaps.

**Strongest objections an expert would raise.**

| # | Objection | Evidence for | Evidence against |
|---|---|---|---|
| O1 | Most figures rest on summarising fetch tools, so the A grades are overstated. | Before assembly, several A grades cited pages read through a summarising fetch tool (for example the Netherlands and France pages); the notes flag these. | The ledger now caps such pages at B, and round 2 re-read the load-bearing figures: GOV.UK threshold page [S3], BMI notice [S94], IND norms page [S113] and newsletter [S114], and the French summaries [S127], [S134] agree with round 1. |
| O2 | The UK settlement response is not yet law, so the thresholds in KF1 may change. | Implementing Statement of Changes not found; WFW describes phased implementation through 2027 [S6] (B). | The response is an official government document on GOV.UK [S7] (A); its figures are stated as the policy. |
| O3 | Ireland's CSEP route is unavailable for AI roles, because the CSOL does not name AI or ML engineers. | CSOL names no data scientist, AI or ML engineer [S64] (A); the GEP fallback carries a labour-market needs test and the 50:50 rule [S69] (A). | Software developers (SOC 2136), IT professionals (2137) and IT professionals n.e.c. including DevOps (2139) are on the CSOL [S64] (A), and the CSEP covers them. |
| O4 | The Dutch 27% ruling and the €50,436 criterion for 2027 may not be enacted. | Ministry of Finance page of 2026-09-15 says plans are final only after both chambers agree and does not mention the ruling [S110] (B). | A summary states the law is adopted [S103] (B). The 2026 criterion (€46,107) is in force per the summary [S103] (B). |
| O5 | The German graduate reduced floor may be €45,934.20 or may not exist as a separate rule. | S86 reads 45.3% of the ceiling for graduates under 3 years [S86] (B). | S84 gives the rule without a figure [S84] (A); the Blue Card floor is in force in the BMI notice [S94] (A) for the reduced case in bottleneck professions. |
| O6 | Processing times are unknown for most routes, so the time column in the recommendations is weak. | The GOV.UK processing-times page was refused [S20]; no dated consular waits were found for any country. | Ireland's embassy decision times [S74] (B), Germany's municipal fast-track times [S88] (A) and the Dutch IND target [S107] (B) give partial figures. |
| O7 | The recommendation for a senior engineer without an offer is thin: most countries show no route. | The Netherlands, Ireland and Switzerland have no job-seeker route in the sources read [S104] (B, scope). | The UK Global Talent route (research and digital) and the German Opportunity Card are the two options with official text read [S42] (A), [S87] (A). |

**Unresolved questions for the coordinator.**

- Which settlement threshold applies to a person already on the Skilled Worker route when the implementing instrument is published (transitional rules not read).
- Whether the Irish GEP Stamp 4 timing (57 months or 5 years) and the CSEP 21-month rule have a statutory or policy basis (not opened).
- Whether the French decree 2026-308 changes the Blue Card requirements (title only).
- The Swiss graduate exemption's in-force date (2022 suggested by headlines, not verified).

## Discovered Concerns

Findings outside the brief's K-claims, or contradictions between sources that a reader should know about.

1. **Dutch 30% ruling status conflict.** [S103] (B summary) says the law is adopted; [S110] (B, Ministry of Finance, 2026-09-15) says the Belastingplan 2027 plans are final only after both chambers agree and does not mention the ruling. The 27% rate and the €50,436 criterion for 2027 are V0 until a Staatsblad entry or chamber vote is found.
2. **Irish Stamp 4 timing.** CSEP: 21 months [S67] (A) against the prior's 2 years; GEP: 57 months [S67] against 5 years [S69]. The 2026 interim measure for a processing backlog [S63] (A) shows that timing can slip beyond the 21-month rule.
3. **Irish CSEP wording.** The Department uses "with / without relevant degree" [S61] (A); a law-firm summary uses CSOL and other occupations [S76] (B). The reader should check the official eligibility text before acting.
4. **Irish intra-company and contract-for-services threshold.** €49,523 from 2026-03-01 appears in one law-firm summary [S76] (B); the enterprise.gov.ie page was refused.
5. **French decree 2026-1001.** Dated 2026-10-26, Journal Officiel 2026-10-29, in force 2026-10-30 [S127] (B summary), [S128] (B copy), [S129] (B). It changes Blue Card validity (tied to contract length), the job-change restriction (12 to 6 months) and the residence-permit decision deadline (4 months to 90 days). It post-dates the round 1 K7 reading and is not mentioned in the earlier French sources.
6. **French minimum-contract conflict.** Six months [S116], [S120]; one year (France-Visas, not opened); none [S127] (B summary), [S129] (B). The official summary is the most recent official text read.
7. **French carte de résident and multi-year card language.** The prior's language split is confirmed on one official page [S133] (B): B1 for the resident card, A2 for the first multi-year card; the round 1 B2 for naturalisation rests on a summary [S122] (B) and is not re-verified.
8. **UK settlement response.** Published in November 2026 [S7] (A), after the K4 premise. The response sets English B2 as the standard for settlement [S7] (A), while the enacted HC 1691 change has a start date of 2027-03-26 [S8] (B). The implementing instrument and transitional terms are not found.
9. **UK Global Talent endorsing body.** Tech Nation is named on earlier GOV.UK reads [S43] (A) but not on the 2026 page [S55] (A). Snippets describe a 2023 Tech Nation closure announcement [S57] (C), a 2024 tender [S56] (B, snippet) and an August 2025 endorsement-process change [S58] (C). The current endorsing body is unverified.
10. **UK Graduate route archive date.** The archived page is graded A, but its capture date is unresolved (between 2026-08-13 and 2026-11-03), and the length change is corroborated only by a commentary source [S17] (C).
11. **UK Temporary Shortage List.** A commentary title says the Immigration Salary List is replaced [S29] (C); GOV.UK still references the Immigration Salary List [S3], [S4] (A). The replacement claim is not confirmed.
12. **Swiss quota split reversed.** The prior said 4,000 B and 4,500 L; the SEM release says 4,500 B and 4,000 L for 2026 [S136] (A). The B permit (multi-year) has the larger quota, and the L permit (short-stay) the smaller. This changes which permit a reader can plan around.
13. **Swiss vote of 14 June 2026.** "No to 10 million Switzerland" was rejected [S138] (A, date), [S140] (C). Basel-Stadt: 18,555 yes (26.52%) and 51,418 no (73.48%) [S139] (B, extract). National totals not verified on an official page.
14. **Netherlands naturalisation.** A draft memorandum proposes raising the standard period from 5 to 10 years, not submitted to parliament, consultation not set [S111] (B). The earlier 2-month consultation detail is not in the document. A media report that the plan was dropped (ARY News, 2026-01-31) is unverified (D).
15. **Netherlands highly-skilled migrant norm.** The prior's about €5,900 is updated to €5,942 for age 30 or over in 2026 [S113], [S114] (B).
16. **German 2026 contribution ceiling.** €101,400 rests on a law-firm summary [S80] (B); the BMI notice [S94] (A) does not state the ceiling, and the floors are consistent with it by arithmetic (M).
17. **German IT-without-degree routes.** The federal graphic lists two routes, not one; the brief's claim covers only the 3-in-7 route [S85] (A, graphic).
18. **German fast-track fee.** €411 is the upper value of a range on one municipal page [S88] (A); other authorities may differ.
19. **Irish Embassy processing units.** The Embassy page uses weeks and months in its table and says "working days" in its footnote [S74] (B); the unit is internally inconsistent, and both readings are recorded in the notes.
20. **UK TB-test metadata.** The archived GOV.UK page shows a metadata update of 2026-10-27 and a public last-updated date of 2015-01-07 [S19] (B archived).
21. **Germany–India 90,000 arrangement.** Media only [S93] (C); a vendor page says the quota was raised without a number [S96] (C). Not an official route in the sources read.
22. **Irish permit statistics.** The 2025 files are published as xlsx and could not be read [S75] (A, existence only). A search headline suggests a possible drop in 2025 permit numbers [S72] (C, headline, not verified).

## Delta

| # | Prior | Status | Evidence | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | UK Skilled Worker: since 22 July 2025 the general threshold is £41,700 a year, minimum RQF 6, and the Immigration Salary List has been replaced by a Temporary Shortage List. | Updated | £41,700 and RQF 6 confirmed on GOV.UK [S3], [S2] (A). The 22 July 2025 effective date is not printed; April 2026 appears in a commentary source [S9] (C). The Immigration Salary List is still referenced [S4] (A); the replacement claim rests on a commentary title [S29] (C) and is not confirmed. | Use £41,700 and the going rate, and do not repeat the effective date or the replacement claim as fact. |
| P2 | Germany: the 2026 EU Blue Card floors are €50,700 and €45,934.20 (50% and 45.3% of the 2026 ceiling of €101,400); the 2027 floors are published in December. | Confirmed | The 2026 floors are confirmed on the BMI notice dated 2025-12-02 [S94] (A). The 2026 ceiling rests on a law-firm summary [S80] (B) and arithmetic. The December 2027 publication cannot be verified; the 2025 notice precedent is dated 2 December [S94]. | Use the 2026 floors. Check the BMI notice for 2027 after December 2026. |
| P3 | Netherlands: the 30% ruling falls to 27% from 1 January 2027; the 2026 highly-skilled migrant threshold for age 30 or over is about €5,900. | Updated | The threshold is €5,942 gross a month [S113], [S114] (B). The 27% rate is in conflict: [S103] (B) says adopted; [S110] (B) says the plans are not final until both chambers agree. | Use €5,942. Treat the 27% rate as V0 until the bill and vote are found. |
| P4 | Ireland: CSEP holders can apply for Stamp 4 after 2 years; 2025 floors €38,000 (CSOL) and €64,000 (other); 2026 figures not recalled. | Updated | Stamp 4 after 21 months from commencement of employment [S67] (A; legal basis Gap). The 2025 values are confirmed in substance; the official labels are "with / without relevant degree". 2026 thresholds: €40,904 and €68,911 from 2026-03-01 [S61], [S70] (A). | Use 21 months and the 2026 thresholds. Note the GEP Stamp 4 conflict. |
| P5 | France: the EU Blue Card floor is about 1.0 times the average gross salary (€55,000–€60,000), separate from the salarié qualifié Talent Passport; language: A2 for the multi-year card and B1 for the resident card from 2026-01-01. | Contradicted | The multiplier is 1.5 times the reference salary, giving €59,373 gross a year [S127] (B), [S134] (B); this contradicts the 1.0 times multiplier. The categories are separate [S134] (B). The A2 multi-year and B1 resident-card levels are confirmed [S133] (B). | Use €59,373 for the Blue Card and €39,582 for salarié qualifié. |
| P6 | Switzerland: 2026 federal quotas were 4,000 B and 4,500 L (8,500 in all, plus UK-specific quotas); no EU Blue Card; the "No to 10 million Switzerland" initiative was due to a popular vote in 2026. | Contradicted | The split is reversed: 4,500 B and 4,000 L; total 8,500 and UK-specific quotas (3,500) confirmed [S136] (A). The vote was held on 2026-06-14 and rejected [S138] (A date), [S139] (B). The EU Blue Card point was not checked on an official page (D). | Plan around 4,500 B and 4,000 L for 2026. The vote is no longer a pending event. |
